# April 15 vs May 4 Qualtrics exports — value-level agreement

Generated by `scripts/audit/compare_april_may.py`. Columns joined by Qualtrics ImportId (QID), never by position. April stores text labels, May stores numeric recode codes; May codes are translated with the production tables in `api/_processing.py` (`_QSF_RECODE_LABELS`, `_COLNAME_RECODE_LABELS`, imported read-only).

## Headline

**The two exports agree. All 82 April responses are present in the May export; May adds 9 newer ones. No response changed its `RecordedDate`, so nobody edited an answer between the exports.**

**Within every one of the 134 joined question columns, each May numeric code corresponds to exactly one April text and each April text to exactly one May code — a perfect bijection over all non-empty compared cells. The two files therefore hold the same answers in two encodings.**

**The 2,556 cells classified DIFFERENT below are therefore NOT export disagreements. They are cases where the production label table in `api/_processing.py` decodes May's code to something other than April's own text — i.e. the disagreement is between the label tables and Qualtrics, which is very likely the cause of the collaborators' report. See section 4.**

## 1. The join (by ResponseId)

| Set | Count |
| --- | --- |
| April data rows | 82 |
| May data rows | 91 |
| April only | 0 |
| May only (new responses collected after Apr 15) | 9 |
| **In BOTH** | **82** |

## 2. Per-verdict summary

Question columns joined by QID: **134** (of which 49 have a label table). Cells compared: 82 responses x 134 columns = 10,988.

| Verdict | Cells | % of all | % of non-empty |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SAME | 1,643 | 14.95% | 21.12% |
| DIFFERENT | 2,556 | 23.26% | 32.85% |
| UNTRANSLATABLE | 3,581 | 32.59% | 46.03% |
| BOTH-EMPTY | 3,208 | 29.20% | - |
| ONE-EMPTY | 0 | 0.00% | - |
| **total** | **10,988** | 100% | |

- SAME = April text equals May code translated (or both hold the same raw string, e.g. free text and numeric-only fields).
- UNTRANSLATABLE = the two cells differ as strings and no label table covers that QID, so agreement cannot be checked either way. This is the audit coverage gap, not evidence of a bug.
- DIFFERENT = the May code translated cleanly and the result is not April's text. Given the bijection in section 3, this means the LABEL TABLE disagrees with April, not that the two exports disagree.

## 3. Bijection test — do the two encodings carry the same data?

For every joined question column, over all cells where both files are non-empty: is the May value <-> April value correspondence one-to-one? This test needs no label table, so it covers the UNTRANSLATABLE columns too. A violation would mean one respondent got two different answers.

| Direction | Violations |
| --- | --- |
| one May code -> several April texts | 0 |
| one April text -> several May codes | 0 |

**Zero violations.** The April and May exports are two faithful encodings of one identical dataset.

## 4. Where the disagreements actually come from

Every DIFFERENT cell falls into one of two kinds:

| Kind | Cells | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PLACEHOLDER | 796 | April's text is an unconfigured Qualtrics placeholder (`Click to write Scale Point N` / `Click to write Choice N`). April carries no readable label for these, so the label table cannot be checked against it — but the code<->text correspondence is still ordinal and consistent. |
| TABLE-MISMAP | 1,760 | April has a real label and the label table decodes May's code to a different real label. These are production translation bugs. |

Per-QID audit of each label table against April's own text. "April text observed" is the single April label that always accompanies that May code (the bijection above guarantees it is unique).

### Interpretation of the mismatches (analyst judgement)

The tables above are measurement. This is judgement, formed by reading each question's verbatim text (CSV header row 1) beside its observed code/text pairs. Classes: **TABLE-BUG** = April's label fits the question and the production table does not, so the dashboard is showing the wrong answer; **APRIL-BAD** = the table fits the question and April's label set does not (labels recycled from another question), so April cannot validate the table and only the QSF can; **WORDING** = same meaning, different words.

| QID | Class | Mismatching cells | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QID124 | **APRIL-BAD** | 707 | Question asks "have you experienced ... Flooding of house ...?" The table's Yes/No/PNA fits it; April's labels are frequencies (Daily/Weekly/Biweekly). |
| QID195 | **APRIL-BAD** | 536 | Like/dislike matrix. April's answer-choice labels hold the intervention STATEMENT list instead of the like/dislike scale, so April cannot say what codes 1/6/7 mean. |
| QID43 | **WORDING** | 225 | Frequency scale. Only the wording differs ("Yearly" vs "annually", "Rarely or Never" vs "Never or rarely"). Same meaning, same order. |
| QID21 | **APRIL-BAD** | 73 | Question asks "Do you feel safe in your house ...?" The table's safety scale fits it; April's labels (Less than 6 months / More than 6 months / Don't know) belong to some other question. April's label set is misconfigured. |
| QID47 | **APRIL-BAD** | 69 | Question asks "How long do you anticipate continuing to live in your current house?" The table's duration scale fits it; April's labels are building materials (Wood/Concrete/Steel). |
| QID194 | **APRIL-BAD** | 65 | Same as QID21 (social-threat twin): table fits the question, April's duration labels do not. |
| QID192 | **TABLE-BUG** | 56 | Question: "When was your house built?" April's labels are a coherent, ordered age scale (Before 1960 / 1960-1979 / 1980-1999 / 2000 or later). The table maps the same codes in the REVERSE order, so every house age is inverted. Feeds _compute_struct_score. |
| QID59 | **TABLE-BUG** | 23 | Question: "Has anyone in your home visited a hospital ... for respiratory health issues?" April shows codes 1/2/3 are all "Yes, ..." variants and 4 is "No". The table maps codes 2 and 3 to "No", flipping 15 households from Yes to No. |
| QID219 | **WORDING** | 6 | Code 4 is the write-in option; April shows its prompt text ("Name the problem"), the table calls it "Other". |

**Confirmed production label-table bugs: 2 (QID192, QID59).** These are not export disagreements; both CSVs agree, and `api/_processing.py` mislabels them. Fixing them is out of scope for this read-only audit.

Leads to verify against the QSF (not conclusions):

- **QID141** — Code 1 has no April label ("Click to write Choice 5") yet the table decodes it as "Excellent- No repairs needed." The placeholder names the FIFTH choice, which in the QSF is "Critical- Uninhabitable without repairs." If code 1 really is choice 5, 11 responses are being shown as the best housing condition when they are the worst. QID192 is confirmed reversed, so an inversion here is plausible. Verify against the QSF.

## 5. Respondent edits vs translation bugs

| RecordedDate between exports | DIFFERENT cells | Reading |
| --- | --- | --- |
| differs | 0 | respondent likely edited their answer between Apr 15 and May 4 |
| identical | 2556 | same submission, unchanged answer — the disagreement is in the label table, not in the data |

Not one of the 82 shared responses changed its `RecordedDate`, so no DIFFERENT cell can be explained as a respondent editing their answer between April 15 and May 4. Every one is a labelling fault. Section 4 splits them into real table bugs, wording differences, and cases where April's own label set is the broken side.
